Signature Decade Snapshot Cards JAH-COTYS-000018 · compounded

Evidence: design-study Signature-made design; all model values are illustrative design values, never presented as real historical fact.

The Signature Decade Snapshot Cards are a Signature design-study for a set of price snapshot cards. They are not a real product and they are not a historical record. The design gives one card per year for the price of a loaf of bread: 1950, 2024, and 2050. The loaf cost $0.14 in 1950, $1.97 in 2024, and $3.47 in the 2050 model. The historical figures are real BLS prices; the 2050 figure is a model value made for the design, and every display of the series must keep the design-study label.

History

The Signature Decade Snapshot Cards are a design for a set of three cards, one per year, that show the price of a loaf of bread: fourteen cents in 1950, $1.97 in 2024, and $3.47 in the 2050 model, where the 1950 and 2024 figures are real BLS prices from the public bread record and only the 2050 figure is a model value, grown from the 2024 anchor at the steady 2.2 percent design rate. Each card shows the price, the price in 2024 dollars, and one line about the bread market of that year. The cards were designed as a Signature companion to the public bread record, for classrooms and exhibits that need a price in the hand.

Description

The Signature Decade Snapshot Cards are a Signature design-study for a set of price snapshot cards. They are not a real product and they are not a historical record. The design gives one card per year for the price of a loaf of bread: 1950, 2024, and 2050. The loaf cost $0.14 in 1950, $1.97 in 2024, and $3.47 in the 2050 model. The historical figures are real BLS prices; the 2050 figure is a model value made for the design, and every display of the series must keep the design-study label.

The Problem

How can the price of bread in three years be shown on three cards, so that a reader holding them can feel the change, without mixing the real prices with the model price?

The Worked Solution

The design gives one card per year, with the price, the price in 2024 dollars, and one line about the bread market of that year. The 1950 and 2024 cards carry the real BLS bread prices from the public record, and the 2050 card carries the model price, grown from the 2024 anchor at the steady 2.2 percent design rate, and is marked as a model on its face. The series is 1950: $0.14 per loaf; 2024: $1.97 per loaf; 2050: $3.47 per loaf. The design projects prices forward from its 2024 anchor at a steady 2.2% nominal rise per year, a Signature design assumption, not a forecast. This is a design-study record: every value here is a model value made for the design, not a real historical price. The demo recomputes the checkable part of this solution from the key figures {"points": 3, "earliest_year": 1950, "latest_year": 2050, "per_loaf_first": 0.14, "per_loaf_last": 3.47, "per_loaf_multiple": 24.79, "per_loaf_cagr": 0.0326}.

The Answer

The model loaf cost $0.14 in 1950, $1.97 in 2024, and $3.47 in 2050. The nominal multiple from 1950 to 2024 is about 14.07, and from 2024 to 2050 about 1.76. In 2024 dollars the 1950 loaf cost about $1.82, so bread became genuinely dearer in real terms from 1950 to 2024, while the 2050 model loaf costs about $1.93 in 2024 dollars, close to the 2024 loaf by design. The series is 1950: $0.14 per loaf; 2024: $1.97 per loaf; 2050: $3.47 per loaf. From 1950 to 2050 the per loaf price rose from $0.14 to $3.47, a multiple of 24.79 and a compound annual growth rate of 3.26% per year.

import hashlib
import math
series = [(1950, 0.14, 'per loaf'), (2024, 1.97, 'per loaf'), (2050, 3.47, 'per loaf')]
cpi = {1930: 16.7, 1940: 14.0, 1950: 24.1, 1960: 29.6, 1970: 38.8, 1980: 82.4, 1990: 130.7, 2000: 172.2, 2010: 218.1, 2020: 258.8, 2024: 313.7, 2030: 381.0, 2040: 464.0, 2050: 564.0}
def compute():
    groups = {}
    for (y, p, u) in series:
        groups.setdefault(u, []).append((y, p))
    out = {}
    for u in sorted(groups):
        pts = groups[u]
        y0, p0 = pts[0]
        y1, p1 = pts[-1]
        key = u.replace(' ', '_').replace('-', '_').replace('/', '_').replace('.', '')
        out[key + '_first'] = p0
        out[key + '_last'] = p1
        out[key + '_multiple'] = round(p1 / p0, 2)
        out[key + '_span_years'] = y1 - y0
        out[key + '_cagr'] = round((p1 / p0) ** (1.0 / (y1 - y0)) - 1.0, 4) if y1 > y0 else 0.0
    conv = {}
    for (y, p, u) in series:
        if y in cpi:
            conv[y] = round(p * 313.7 / cpi[y], 2)
    out['in_2024_dollars'] = conv
    out['earliest_year'] = series[0][0]
    out['latest_year'] = series[-1][0]
    out['points'] = len(series)
    return out
outputs = compute()
result = "|".join(f"{k}={v}" for k, v in sorted(outputs.items()))
h1 = hashlib.sha256(result.encode()).hexdigest()
outputs2 = compute()
result2 = "|".join(f"{k}={v}" for k, v in sorted(outputs2.items()))
h2 = hashlib.sha256(result2.encode()).hexdigest()
for k, v in sorted(outputs.items()):
    print(f"{k}: {v}")
print(f"Result: {result}")
print(f"Run 1 SHA-256: {h1}")
print(f"Run 2 SHA-256: {h2}")
print(f"Deterministic: {'MATCH' if h1 == h2 else 'MISMATCH'}")

import hashlib
class SignatureSnapshotCardsProgram:
    """Working program for this record; run() recomputes the demo result."""
    def run(self):
        import hashlib
        import math
        series = [(1950, 0.14, 'per loaf'), (2024, 1.97, 'per loaf'), (2050, 3.47, 'per loaf')]
        cpi = {1930: 16.7, 1940: 14.0, 1950: 24.1, 1960: 29.6, 1970: 38.8, 1980: 82.4, 1990: 130.7, 2000: 172.2, 2010: 218.1, 2020: 258.8, 2024: 313.7, 2030: 381.0, 2040: 464.0, 2050: 564.0}
        def compute():
            groups = {}
            for (y, p, u) in series:
                groups.setdefault(u, []).append((y, p))
            out = {}
            for u in sorted(groups):
                pts = groups[u]
                y0, p0 = pts[0]
                y1, p1 = pts[-1]
                key = u.replace(' ', '_').replace('-', '_').replace('/', '_').replace('.', '')
                out[key + '_first'] = p0
                out[key + '_last'] = p1
                out[key + '_multiple'] = round(p1 / p0, 2)
                out[key + '_span_years'] = y1 - y0
                out[key + '_cagr'] = round((p1 / p0) ** (1.0 / (y1 - y0)) - 1.0, 4) if y1 > y0 else 0.0
            conv = {}
            for (y, p, u) in series:
                if y in cpi:
                    conv[y] = round(p * 313.7 / cpi[y], 2)
            out['in_2024_dollars'] = conv
            out['earliest_year'] = series[0][0]
            out['latest_year'] = series[-1][0]
            out['points'] = len(series)
            return out
        outputs = compute()
        result = "|".join(f"{k}={v}" for k, v in sorted(outputs.items()))
        h1 = hashlib.sha256(result.encode()).hexdigest()
        outputs2 = compute()
        result2 = "|".join(f"{k}={v}" for k, v in sorted(outputs2.items()))
        h2 = hashlib.sha256(result2.encode()).hexdigest()
        for k, v in sorted(outputs.items()):
            print(f"{k}: {v}")
        print(f"Result: {result}")
        print(f"Run 1 SHA-256: {h1}")
        print(f"Run 2 SHA-256: {h2}")
        print(f"Deterministic: {'MATCH' if h1 == h2 else 'MISMATCH'}")
        return {"deterministic": h1 == h2, "result": result}
if __name__ == "__main__":
    print(SignatureSnapshotCardsProgram().run())

Signature Price Comparison Wall JAH-COTYS-000019 · compounded

Evidence: design-study Signature-made design; all model values are illustrative design values, never presented as real historical fact.

The Signature Price Comparison Wall is a Signature design-study for a price-comparison wall. It is not a real product and it is not a historical record. The design shows a small basket of three groceries, bread, eggs, and bacon, with each item's price in each year and the total in each year, in nominal dollars and in 2024 dollars. The model basket cost $9.12 in 2024, $10.39 in 2030, $12.92 in 2040, and $16.06 in 2050. Every value in this record is a model value made for the design, and every display of the series must keep the design-study label.

History

The Signature Price Comparison Wall is a design for a wall, in a classroom or a museum, that compares the price of a small grocery basket across the years: a loaf of bread, a dozen eggs, and a pound of bacon, which cost $9.12 in the 2024 model anchor, $10.39 in 2030, $12.92 in 2040, and $16.06 in 2050, all grown at the steady 2.2 percent design rate. The wall shows each item's price in each year, the total in each year, and the total in 2024 dollars, so a visitor can see which item drove the rise. The wall was designed as a Signature companion to the public grocery records.

Description

The Signature Price Comparison Wall is a Signature design-study for a price-comparison wall. It is not a real product and it is not a historical record. The design shows a small basket of three groceries, bread, eggs, and bacon, with each item's price in each year and the total in each year, in nominal dollars and in 2024 dollars. The model basket cost $9.12 in 2024, $10.39 in 2030, $12.92 in 2040, and $16.06 in 2050. Every value in this record is a model value made for the design, and every display of the series must keep the design-study label.

The Problem

How can three grocery prices be compared across the years on one wall, so that a visitor sees at a glance which item rose fastest and which fell in real terms, without mistaking the model years for measured prices?

The Worked Solution

The design fixes one basket of three groceries, prices each item for each year, and shows the total in nominal dollars and in 2024 dollars, using the CPI-U for real years and the Signature model deflator for projected years. The historical prices come from the public records, and the model years are grown from the 2024 anchor at the steady 2.2 percent design rate and marked as model on the wall. The series is 2024: $9.12 per basket; 2030: $10.39 per basket; 2040: $12.92 per basket; 2050: $16.06 per basket. The design projects prices forward from its 2024 anchor at a steady 2.2% nominal rise per year, a Signature design assumption, not a forecast. This is a design-study record: every value here is a model value made for the design, not a real historical price. The demo recomputes the checkable part of this solution from the key figures {"points": 4, "earliest_year": 2024, "latest_year": 2050, "per_basket_first": 9.12, "per_basket_last": 16.06, "per_basket_multiple": 1.76, "per_basket_cagr": 0.022}.

The Answer

The model basket cost $9.12 in 2024 and $16.06 in 2050, a nominal multiple of about 1.76 over twenty-six years, which is a compound annual growth rate of about 2.2 percent, by construction. In 2024 dollars the 2050 model basket costs about $8.93, so the model says the basket stays almost exactly as dear in real terms, because the model rate and the model deflator are nearly the same by design. The series is 2024: $9.12 per basket; 2030: $10.39 per basket; 2040: $12.92 per basket; 2050: $16.06 per basket. From 2024 to 2050 the per basket price rose from $9.12 to $16.06, a multiple of 1.76 and a compound annual growth rate of 2.2% per year.

When It Applies

When designing a price tool, a classroom exhibit, or a then-versus-now display that needs a clean, labeled model series to work from.

Limits

As a design-study, this record proves nothing about real future prices. The steady 2.2% model rise is a smooth design assumption; real prices jump, stall, and fall, and no model series here should be quoted as a forecast. As a design-study, this record proves nothing about real future prices. The steady 2.2 percent model rise is a smooth design assumption; real prices jump, stall, and fall, and no model series here should be quoted as a forecast. A real wall would also need to show that bacon fell in real terms for most of the century while eggs rose, and that the basket's total hides those opposite moves.

Math Proof

The same arithmetic as the public records, applied to the design's model series: the nominal multiple is M = P_last / P_first; the compound annual growth rate over N years is CAGR = M^(1/N) - 1; a model price restated in 2024 dollars is P_2024 = P_year x 313.7 / D_year, where D_year is the Signature model deflator for that year (2024: 313.7; 2030: 381.0; 2040: 464.0; 2050: 564.0) or the real CPI-U for a historical anchor year. From 2024 to 2050 the per basket price rose from $9.12 to $16.06, a multiple of 1.76 and a compound annual growth rate of 2.2% per year. The demo recomputes all three from the key figures and prints them twice with SHA-256 digests. Formal statement: P_2024 = P_year * 313.7 / D_year; CAGR = (P_last / P_first)^(1/N) - 1. P_year is the model price in the stated year and unit; D_year is the Signature model deflator for that year, or the real CPI-U annual average for a historical anchor year; 313.7 is the CPI-U annual average for 2024; N is the number of years between the first and last model price in the same unit.

Science Proof

The design uses the CPI-U as its reference frame: the real CPI-U annual averages for historical anchor years, and the Signature model deflator for projected years. The deflator is a design assumption, stated in the method, not a measurement and not a forecast. The deflator is a design assumption, stated in the method, not a measurement and not a forecast. The demo recomputes the nominal multiple, the compound annual growth rate, and the 2024-dollar restatement for every model price from the key figures. The cited background sources confirm the real historical anchors the design builds on; the model values themselves are checked only for internal consistency.

import hashlib
import math
series = [(2024, 9.12, 'per basket'), (2030, 10.39, 'per basket'), (2040, 12.92, 'per basket'), (2050, 16.06, 'per basket')]
cpi = {1930: 16.7, 1940: 14.0, 1950: 24.1, 1960: 29.6, 1970: 38.8, 1980: 82.4, 1990: 130.7, 2000: 172.2, 2010: 218.1, 2020: 258.8, 2024: 313.7, 2030: 381.0, 2040: 464.0, 2050: 564.0}
def compute():
    groups = {}
    for (y, p, u) in series:
        groups.setdefault(u, []).append((y, p))
    out = {}
    for u in sorted(groups):
        pts = groups[u]
        y0, p0 = pts[0]
        y1, p1 = pts[-1]
        key = u.replace(' ', '_').replace('-', '_').replace('/', '_').replace('.', '')
        out[key + '_first'] = p0
        out[key + '_last'] = p1
        out[key + '_multiple'] = round(p1 / p0, 2)
        out[key + '_span_years'] = y1 - y0
        out[key + '_cagr'] = round((p1 / p0) ** (1.0 / (y1 - y0)) - 1.0, 4) if y1 > y0 else 0.0
    conv = {}
    for (y, p, u) in series:
        if y in cpi:
            conv[y] = round(p * 313.7 / cpi[y], 2)
    out['in_2024_dollars'] = conv
    out['earliest_year'] = series[0][0]
    out['latest_year'] = series[-1][0]
    out['points'] = len(series)
    return out
outputs = compute()

Full working program

import hashlib
class SignatureComparisonWallProgram:
    """Working program for this record; run() recomputes the demo result."""
    def run(self):
        import hashlib
        import math
        series = [(2024, 9.12, 'per basket'), (2030, 10.39, 'per basket'), (2040, 12.92, 'per basket'), (2050, 16.06, 'per basket')]
        cpi = {1930: 16.7, 1940: 14.0, 1950: 24.1, 1960: 29.6, 1970: 38.8, 1980: 82.4, 1990: 130.7, 2000: 172.2, 2010: 218.1, 2020: 258.8, 2024: 313.7, 2030: 381.0, 2040: 464.0, 2050: 564.0}
        def compute():
            groups = {}
            for (y, p, u) in series:
                groups.setdefault(u, []).append((y, p))
            out = {}
            for u in sorted(groups):
                pts = groups[u]
                y0, p0 = pts[0]
                y1, p1 = pts[-1]
                key = u.replace(' ', '_').replace('-', '_').replace('/', '_').replace('.', '')
                out[key + '_first'] = p0
                out[key + '_last'] = p1
                out[key + '_multiple'] = round(p1 / p0, 2)
                out[key + '_span_years'] = y1 - y0
                out[key + '_cagr'] = round((p1 / p0) ** (1.0 / (y1 - y0)) - 1.0, 4) if y1 > y0 else 0.0
            conv = {}
            for (y, p, u) in series:
                if y in cpi:
                    conv[y] = round(p * 313.7 / cpi[y], 2)
            out['in_2024_dollars'] = conv
            out['earliest_year'] = series[0][0]
            out['latest_year'] = series[-1][0]
            out['points'] = len(series)
            return out
        outputs = compute()
        result = "|".join(f"{k}={v}" for k, v in sorted(outputs.items()))
        h1 = hashlib.sha256(result.encode()).hexdigest()
        outputs2 = compute()
        result2 = "|".join(f"{k}={v}" for k, v in sorted(outputs2.items()))
        h2 = hashlib.sha256(result2.encode()).hexdigest()
        for k, v in sorted(outputs.items()):
            print(f"{k}: {v}")
        print(f"Result: {result}")
        print(f"Run 1 SHA-256: {h1}")
        print(f"Run 2 SHA-256: {h2}")
        print(f"Deterministic: {'MATCH' if h1 == h2 else 'MISMATCH'}")
        return {"deterministic": h1 == h2, "result": result}
if __name__ == "__main__":
    print(SignatureComparisonWallProgram().run())

Signature Cost Diary JAH-COTYS-000020 · compounded

Evidence: design-study Signature-made design; all model values are illustrative design values, never presented as real historical fact.

The Signature Cost Diary is a Signature design-study for a cost diary. It is not a real product and it is not a historical record. The design records one entry per month, with the total grocery spending, the total in 2024 dollars, and the three items that moved most, so the household can see what drove the change. The model diary spent $374.40 a month in 2024, $426.62 in 2030, $530.33 in 2040, and $659.26 in 2050. Every value in this record is a model value made for the design, and every display of the series must keep the design-study label.

History

The Signature Cost Diary is a design for a diary that records a household's monthly grocery spending: $374.40 a month in the 2024 model anchor, $426.62 in 2030, $530.33 in 2040, and $659.26 in 2050, all grown at the steady 2.2 percent design rate. Each month's entry shows the total, the total in 2024 dollars, and the three items that moved most, so the household can see whether the rise came from meat, dairy, or bread. The diary was designed as a Signature companion to the public grocery records, which hold the real prices its entries are built from.

Description

The Signature Cost Diary is a Signature design-study for a cost diary. It is not a real product and it is not a historical record. The design records one entry per month, with the total grocery spending, the total in 2024 dollars, and the three items that moved most, so the household can see what drove the change. The model diary spent $374.40 a month in 2024, $426.62 in 2030, $530.33 in 2040, and $659.26 in 2050. Every value in this record is a model value made for the design, and every display of the series must keep the design-study label.

The Problem

How can a household keep a diary of what groceries cost, so that a year's entries show which items drove the rise, and how can the model years be entered without looking like a record of spending that happened?

The Worked Solution

The design records one entry per month, with the total, the total in 2024 dollars, and the three items that moved most. The historical entries use the real BLS prices from the public records, and the model entries use the model prices, grown from the 2024 anchor at the steady 2.2 percent design rate, and are marked as model on every page. The series is 2024: $374.40 per month; 2030: $426.62 per month; 2040: $530.33 per month; 2050: $659.26 per month. The Answer

The model diary spent $374.40 a month in 2024 and $659.26 in 2050, a nominal multiple of about 1.76 over twenty-six years, which is a compound annual growth rate of about 2.2 percent, by construction. History

The Signature Cost Diary is a design for a diary that records a household's monthly grocery spending: $374.40 a month in the 2024 model anchor, $426.62 in 2030, $530.33 in 2040, and $659.26 in 2050, all grown at the steady 2.2 percent design rate. Each month's entry shows the total, the total in 2024 dollars, and the three items that moved most, so the household can see whether the rise came from meat, dairy, or bread. The diary was designed as a Signature companion to the public grocery records, which hold the real prices its entries are built from.

Problem

How can a household keep a diary of what groceries cost, so that a year's entries show which items drove the rise, and how can the model years be entered without looking like a record of spending that happened?

Method

The design records one entry per month, with the total, the total in 2024 dollars, and the three items that moved most. The historical entries use the real BLS prices from the public records, and the model entries use the model prices, grown from the 2024 anchor at the steady 2.2 percent design rate, and are marked as model on every page. The series is 2024: $374.40 per month; 2030: $426.62 per month; 2040: $530.33 per month; 2050: $659.26 per month. The design projects prices forward from its 2024 anchor at a steady 2.2% nominal rise per year, a Signature design assumption, not a forecast. This is a design-study record: every value here is a model value made for the design, not a real historical price.

Dataset

Signature model series, design-study values; historical anchors from the cited public background sources.

Working demo program

import hashlib
import math
series = [(2024, 374.4, 'per month'), (2030, 426.62, 'per month'), (2040, 530.33, 'per month'), (2050, 659.26, 'per month')]
cpi = {1930: 16.7, 1940: 14.0, 1950: 24.1, 1960: 29.6, 1970: 38.8, 1980: 82.4, 1990: 130.7, 2000: 172.2, 2010: 218.1, 2020: 258.8, 2024: 313.7, 2030: 381.0, 2040: 464.0, 2050: 564.0}
def compute():
    groups = {}
    for (y, p, u) in series:
        groups.setdefault(u, []).append((y, p))
    out = {}
    for u in sorted(groups):
        pts = groups[u]
        y0, p0 = pts[0]
        y1, p1 = pts[-1]
        key = u.replace(' ', '_').replace('-', '_').replace('/', '_').replace('.', '')
        out[key + '_first'] = p0
        out[key + '_last'] = p1
        out[key + '_multiple'] = round(p1 / p0, 2)
        out[key + '_span_years'] = y1 - y0
        out[key + '_cagr'] = round((p1 / p0) ** (1.0 / (y1 - y0)) - 1.0, 4) if y1 > y0 else 0.0
    conv = {}
    for (y, p, u) in series:
        if y in cpi:
            conv[y] = round(p * 313.7 / cpi[y], 2)
    out['in_2024_dollars'] = conv
    out['earliest_year'] = series[0][0]
    out['latest_year'] = series[-1][0]
    out['points'] = len(series)
    return out
outputs = compute()
result = "|".join(f"{k}={v}" for k, v in sorted(outputs.items()))
h1 = hashlib.sha256(result.encode()).hexdigest()
outputs2 = compute()
result2 = "|".join(f"{k}={v}" for k, v in sorted(outputs2.items()))
h2 = hashlib.sha256(result2.encode()).hexdigest()
for k, v in sorted(outputs.items()):
    print(f"{k}: {v}")
print(f"Result: {result}")
print(f"Run 1 SHA-256: {h1}")
print(f"Run 2 SHA-256: {h2}")
print(f"Deterministic: {'MATCH' if h1 == h2 else 'MISMATCH'}")

Full working program

import hashlib
class SignatureCostDiaryProgram:
    """Working program for this record; run() recomputes the demo result."""
    def run(self):
        import hashlib
        import math
        series = [(2024, 374.4, 'per month'), (2030, 426.62, 'per month'), (2040, 530.33, 'per month'), (2050, 659.26, 'per month')]
        cpi = {1930: 16.7, 1940: 14.0, 1950: 24.1, 1960: 29.6, 1970: 38.8, 1980: 82.4, 1990: 130.7, 2000: 172.2, 2010: 218.1, 2020: 258.8, 2024: 313.7, 2030: 381.0, 2040: 464.0, 2050: 564.0}
        def compute():
            groups = {}
            for (y, p, u) in series:
                groups.setdefault(u, []).append((y, p))
            out = {}
            for u in sorted(groups):
                pts = groups[u]
                y0, p0 = pts[0]
                y1, p1 = pts[-1]
                key = u.replace(' ', '_').replace('-', '_').replace('/', '_').replace('.', '')
                out[key + '_first'] = p0
                out[key + '_last'] = p1
                out[key + '_multiple'] = round(p1 / p0, 2)
                out[key + '_span_years'] = y1 - y0
                out[key + '_cagr'] = round((p1 / p0) ** (1.0 / (y1 - y0)) - 1.0, 4) if y1 > y0 else 0.0
            conv = {}
            for (y, p, u) in series:
                if y in cpi:
                    conv[y] = round(p * 313.7 / cpi[y], 2)
            out['in_2024_dollars'] = conv
            out['earliest_year'] = series[0][0]
            out['latest_year'] = series[-1][0]
            out['points'] = len(series)
            return out
        outputs = compute()
        result = "|".join(f"{k}={v}" for k, v in sorted(outputs.items()))
        h1 = hashlib.sha256(result.encode()).hexdigest()
        outputs2 = compute()
        result2 = "|".join(f"{k}={v}" for k, v in sorted(outputs2.items()))
        h2 = hashlib.sha256(result2.encode()).hexdigest()
        for k, v in sorted(outputs.items()):
            print(f"{k}: {v}")
        print(f"Result: {result}")
        print(f"Run 1 SHA-256: {h1}")
        print(f"Run 2 SHA-256: {h2}")
        print(f"Deterministic: {'MATCH' if h1 == h2 else 'MISMATCH'}")
        return {"deterministic": h1 == h2, "result": result}
if __name__ == "__main__":
    print(SignatureCostDiaryProgram().run())

Signature Future Grocery Store JAH-COTYS-000021 · compounded

Evidence: design-study Signature-made design; all model values are illustrative design values, never presented as real historical fact.

The Signature Future Grocery Store is a Signature design-study for a grocery store of 2050. It is not a real product, it is not a forecast, and it is not a historical record. The design lays out a store with eight shelves, one per grocery, and prices each shelf in 2024 and in the model year 2050, with the model prices grown from the 2024 anchor at the steady 2.2 percent design rate. The model basket cost $124.60 in 2024, $141.98 in 2030, $176.49 in 2040, and $219.40 in 2050. Every value in this record is a model value made for the design, and every display of the series must keep the design-study label.

History

The Signature Future Grocery Store is a design for a grocery store of 2050, with a model basket priced for that year: the basket cost $124.60 in 2024, $141.98 in 2030, $176.49 in 2040, and $219.40 in 2050, all grown at the steady 2.2 percent design rate. The store's shelves hold the same groceries as the public records: bread, milk, eggs, bacon, ground beef, butter, cheddar, and sugar, and each shelf shows the price in 2024 and the model price in 2050, so a visitor can walk the store and see what the model says the future costs. The store was designed as a Signature companion to the public grocery records.

Description

The Signature Future Grocery Store is a Signature design-study for a grocery store of 2050. It is not a real product, it is not a forecast, and it is not a historical record. The Problem

How can a grocery store of the future be designed, so that a visitor can walk its shelves and see what the model says each grocery will cost in 2050, without mistaking the model for a prediction?

The design fixes one basket of eight groceries from the public records, prices each item from the real BLS prices for 2024, grows each price forward to 2050 at the steady 2.2 percent design rate, and shows both prices on each shelf, with the 2050 price marked as a model on its face.   • care: Never present a model value as a real historical price or a forecast; keep the design-study label on every display of the series.

Boundless scales

  • A1: One 1 Signature future grocery store in the design: what the model charges and why.
  • A2: The whole model series: the design across its decades.
  • A3: The design against the whole economy: model prices restated in 2024 dollars.

Key figures

  • points: 4
  • earliest_year: 2024
  • latest_year: 2050
  • per_basket_first: 124.6
  • per_basket_last: 219.4
  • per_basket_multiple: 1.76
  • per_basket_cagr: 0.022

Working demo program

import hashlib
import math
series = [(2024, 124.6, 'per basket'), (2030, 141.98, 'per basket'), (2040, 176.49, 'per basket'), (2050, 219.4, 'per basket')]
cpi = {1930: 16.7, 1940: 14.0, 1950: 24.1, 1960: 29.6, 1970: 38.8, 1980: 82.4, 1990: 130.7, 2000: 172.2, 2010: 218.1, 2020: 258.8, 2024: 313.7, 2030: 381.0, 2040: 464.0, 2050: 564.0}
def compute():
    groups = {}
    for (y, p, u) in series:
        groups.setdefault(u, []).append((y, p))
    out = {}
    for u in sorted(groups):
        pts = groups[u]
        y0, p0 = pts[0]
        y1, p1 = pts[-1]
        key = u.replace(' ', '_').replace('-', '_').replace('/', '_').replace('.', '')
        out[key + '_first'] = p0
        out[key + '_last'] = p1
        out[key + '_multiple'] = round(p1 / p0, 2)
        out[key + '_span_years'] = y1 - y0
        out[key + '_cagr'] = round((p1 / p0) ** (1.0 / (y1 - y0)) - 1.0, 4) if y1 > y0 else 0.0
    conv = {}
    for (y, p, u) in series:
        if y in cpi:
            conv[y] = round(p * 313.7 / cpi[y], 2)
    out['in_2024_dollars'] = conv
    out['earliest_year'] = series[0][0]
    out['latest_year'] = series[-1][0]
    out['points'] = len(series)
    return out
outputs = compute()
result = "|".join(f"{k}={v}" for k, v in sorted(outputs.items()))
h1 = hashlib.sha256(result.encode()).hexdigest()
outputs2 = compute()
result2 = "|".join(f"{k}={v}" for k, v in sorted(outputs2.items()))
h2 = hashlib.sha256(result2.encode()).hexdigest()
for k, v in sorted(outputs.items()):
    print(f"{k}: {v}")
print(f"Result: {result}")
print(f"Run 1 SHA-256: {h1}")
print(f"Run 2 SHA-256: {h2}")
print(f"Deterministic: {'MATCH' if h1 == h2 else 'MISMATCH'}")

class SignatureFutureGroceryProgram:
    """Working program for this record; run() recomputes the demo result."""
    def run(self):
        import hashlib
        import math
        series = [(2024, 124.6, 'per basket'), (2030, 141.98, 'per basket'), (2040, 176.49, 'per basket'), (2050, 219.4, 'per basket')]
        cpi = {1930: 16.7, 1940: 14.0, 1950: 24.1, 1960: 29.6, 1970: 38.8, 1980: 82.4, 1990: 130.7, 2000: 172.2, 2010: 218.1, 2020: 258.8, 2024: 313.7, 2030: 381.0, 2040: 464.0, 2050: 564.0}
        def compute():
            groups = {}
            for (y, p, u) in series:
                groups.setdefault(u, []).append((y, p))
            out = {}
            for u in sorted(groups):
                pts = groups[u]
                y0, p0 = pts[0]
                y1, p1 = pts[-1]
                key = u.replace(' ', '_').replace('-', '_').replace('/', '_').replace('.', '')
                out[key + '_first'] = p0
                out[key + '_last'] = p1
                out[key + '_multiple'] = round(p1 / p0, 2)
                out[key + '_span_years'] = y1 - y0
                out[key + '_cagr'] = round((p1 / p0) ** (1.0 / (y1 - y0)) - 1.0, 4) if y1 > y0 else 0.0
            conv = {}
            for (y, p, u) in series:
                if y in cpi:
                    conv[y] = round(p * 313.7 / cpi[y], 2)
            out['in_2024_dollars'] = conv
            out['earliest_year'] = series[0][0]
            out['latest_year'] = series[-1][0]
            out['points'] = len(series)
            return out
        outputs = compute()
        result = "|".join(f"{k}={v}" for k, v in sorted(outputs.items()))
        h1 = hashlib.sha256(result.encode()).hexdigest()
        outputs2 = compute()
        result2 = "|".join(f"{k}={v}" for k, v in sorted(outputs2.items()))
        h2 = hashlib.sha256(result2.encode()).hexdigest()
        for k, v in sorted(outputs.items()):
            print(f"{k}: {v}")
        print(f"Result: {result}")
        print(f"Run 1 SHA-256: {h1}")
        print(f"Run 2 SHA-256: {h2}")
        print(f"Deterministic: {'MATCH' if h1 == h2 else 'MISMATCH'}")
        return {"deterministic": h1 == h2, "result": result}
if __name__ == "__main__":
    print(SignatureFutureGroceryProgram().run())

Signature Bread 2050 Cost Design JAH-COTYS-000022 · compounded

Evidence: design-study Signature-made design; all model values are illustrative design values, never presented as real historical fact.

The Signature Bread 2050 Cost Design is a Signature design-study for the cost of a loaf of bread in 2050. It is not a real product, it is not a forecast, and it is not a historical record. The design takes the real BLS bread price of $1.97 in 2024 and grows it forward at the steady 2.2 percent design rate, giving $2.24 in 2030, $2.79 in 2040, and $3.47 in 2050. Every value in this record is a model value made for the design, and every display of the series must keep the design-study label.

History

The Signature Bread 2050 Cost Design is a design for what a loaf of bread would cost in 2050 under a steady model rate: $1.97 in 2024, $2.24 in 2030, $2.79 in 2040, and $3.47 in 2050, all grown at the steady 2.2 percent design rate. The design shows the real bread history beside the model, from nine cents in 1930 to $1.97 in 2024, so a reader can see that the model rate of 2.2 percent is slower than the real rate of about 3.4 percent a year that bread actually rose at over the last ninety-four years. The design was made as a Signature companion to the public bread record.

Description

The Signature Bread 2050 Cost Design is a Signature design-study for the cost of a loaf of bread in 2050. It is not a real product, it is not a forecast, and it is not a historical record. The design takes the real BLS bread price of $1.97 in 2024 and grows it forward at the steady 2.2 percent design rate, giving $2.24 in 2030, $2.79 in 2040, and $3.47 in 2050. Every value in this record is a model value made for the design, and every display of the series must keep the design-study label.

The Problem

What would a loaf of bread cost in 2050 if it rose at a steady 2.2 percent a year from 2024, and how does that compare with the rate bread actually rose at over the last ninety-four years?

The Worked Solution

The design takes the real BLS bread price of $1.97 in 2024 and grows it forward at the steady 2.2 percent design rate, giving $2.24 in 2030, $2.79 in 2040, and $3.47 in 2050. The design shows the real bread history beside the model, from nine cents in 1930 to $1.97 in 2024, and notes that the real rate over that span was about 3.4 percent a year, faster than the model rate. The series is 2024: $1.97 per loaf; 2030: $2.24 per loaf; 2040: $2.79 per loaf; 2050: $3.47 per loaf. The design projects prices forward from its 2024 anchor at a steady 2.2% nominal rise per year, a Signature design assumption, not a forecast. This is a design-study record: every value here is a model value made for the design, not a real historical price. The demo recomputes the checkable part of this solution from the key figures {"points": 4, "earliest_year": 2024, "latest_year": 2050, "per_loaf_first": 1.97, "per_loaf_last": 3.47, "per_loaf_multiple": 1.76, "per_loaf_cagr": 0.022}.

The Answer

The model loaf cost $1.97 in 2024 and $3.47 in 2050, a nominal multiple of about 1.76 over twenty-six years, which is a compound annual growth rate of about 2.2 percent, by construction. In 2024 dollars the 2050 model loaf costs about $1.93, so the model says the loaf stays almost exactly as dear in real terms, because the model rate and the model deflator are nearly the same by design. The series is 2024: $1.97 per loaf; 2030: $2.24 per loaf; 2040: $2.79 per loaf; 2050: $3.47 per loaf. From 2024 to 2050 the per loaf price rose from $1.97 to $3.47, a multiple of 1.76 and a compound annual growth rate of 2.2% per year.

When It Applies

When designing a price tool, a classroom exhibit, or a then-versus-now display that needs a clean, labeled model series to work from.

Limits

As a design-study, this record proves nothing about real future prices. The steady 2.2% model rise is a smooth design assumption; real prices jump, stall, and fall, and no model series here should be quoted as a forecast. As a design-study, this record proves nothing about real future prices. The steady 2.2 percent model rise is a smooth design assumption; real prices jump, stall, and fall, and no model series here should be quoted as a forecast. The real bread rate of about 3.4 percent a year over 1930-2024 is itself an average over wars, shortages, and booms, and no single rate describes any single decade.

Math Proof

The same arithmetic as the public records, applied to the design's model series: the nominal multiple is M = P_last / P_first; the compound annual growth rate over N years is CAGR = M^(1/N) - 1; a model price restated in 2024 dollars is P_2024 = P_year x 313.7 / D_year, where D_year is the Signature model deflator for that year (2024: 313.7; 2030: 381.0; 2040: 464.0; 2050: 564.0) or the real CPI-U for a historical anchor year. From 2024 to 2050 the per loaf price rose from $1.97 to $3.47, a multiple of 1.76 and a compound annual growth rate of 2.2% per year. The demo recomputes all three from the key figures and prints them twice with SHA-256 digests. Formal statement: P_2024 = P_year * 313.7 / D_year; CAGR = (P_last / P_first)^(1/N) - 1. P_year is the model price in the stated year and unit; D_year is the Signature model deflator for that year, or the real CPI-U annual average for a historical anchor year; 313.7 is the CPI-U annual average for 2024; N is the number of years between the first and last model price in the same unit.

Science Proof

The design uses the CPI-U as its reference frame: the real CPI-U annual averages for historical anchor years, and the Signature model deflator for projected years. The deflator is a design assumption, stated in the method, not a measurement and not a forecast. Full working program

import hashlib
class SignatureBread2050Program:
    """Working program for this record; run() recomputes the demo result."""
    def run(self):
        import hashlib
        import math
        series = [(2024, 1.97, 'per loaf'), (2030, 2.24, 'per loaf'), (2040, 2.79, 'per loaf'), (2050, 3.47, 'per loaf')]
        cpi = {1930: 16.7, 1940: 14.0, 1950: 24.1, 1960: 29.6, 1970: 38.8, 1980: 82.4, 1990: 130.7, 2000: 172.2, 2010: 218.1, 2020: 258.8, 2024: 313.7, 2030: 381.0, 2040: 464.0, 2050: 564.0}
        def compute():
            groups = {}
            for (y, p, u) in series:
                groups.setdefault(u, []).append((y, p))
            out = {}
            for u in sorted(groups):
                pts = groups[u]
                y0, p0 = pts[0]
                y1, p1 = pts[-1]
                key = u.replace(' ', '_').replace('-', '_').replace('/', '_').replace('.', '')
                out[key + '_first'] = p0
                out[key + '_last'] = p1
                out[key + '_multiple'] = round(p1 / p0, 2)
                out[key + '_span_years'] = y1 - y0
                out[key + '_cagr'] = round((p1 / p0) ** (1.0 / (y1 - y0)) - 1.0, 4) if y1 > y0 else 0.0
            conv = {}
            for (y, p, u) in series:
                if y in cpi:
                    conv[y] = round(p * 313.7 / cpi[y], 2)
            out['in_2024_dollars'] = conv
            out['earliest_year'] = series[0][0]
            out['latest_year'] = series[-1][0]
            out['points'] = len(series)
            return out
        outputs = compute()
        result = "|".join(f"{k}={v}" for k, v in sorted(outputs.items()))
        h1 = hashlib.sha256(result.encode()).hexdigest()
        outputs2 = compute()
        result2 = "|".join(f"{k}={v}" for k, v in sorted(outputs2.items()))
        h2 = hashlib.sha256(result2.encode()).hexdigest()
        for k, v in sorted(outputs.items()):
            print(f"{k}: {v}")
        print(f"Result: {result}")
        print(f"Run 1 SHA-256: {h1}")
        print(f"Run 2 SHA-256: {h2}")
        print(f"Deterministic: {'MATCH' if h1 == h2 else 'MISMATCH'}")
        return {"deterministic": h1 == h2, "result": result}
if __name__ == "__main__":
    print(SignatureBread2050Program().run())

Signature House 2050 Cost Design JAH-COTYS-000023 · compounded

Evidence: design-study Signature-made design; all model values are illustrative design values, never presented as real historical fact.

The Signature House 2050 Cost Design is a Signature design-study for the cost of a median new house in 2050. It is not a real product, it is not a forecast, and it is not a historical record. The design takes the real Census median new-home price of $420,300 in 2024 and grows it forward at the steady 2.2 percent design rate, giving $478,921.97 in 2030, $595,351.87 in 2040, and $740,086.84 in 2050. Every value in this record is a model value made for the design, and every display of the series must keep the design-study label.

History

The Signature House 2050 Cost Design is a design for what a median new house would cost in 2050 under a steady model rate: $420,300 in 2024, $478,921.97 in 2030, $595,351.87 in 2040, and $740,086.84 in 2050, all grown at the steady 2.2 percent design rate. The design shows the real house history beside the model, from $18,000 in 1963 to $420,300 in 2024, so a reader can see that the model rate of 2.2 percent is slower than the real rate of about 5.3 percent a year that new houses actually rose at over the last sixty-one years. The design was made as a Signature companion to the public median-new-house record.

Description

The Signature House 2050 Cost Design is a Signature design-study for the cost of a median new house in 2050. It is not a real product, it is not a forecast, and it is not a historical record. The design takes the real Census median new-home price of $420,300 in 2024 and grows it forward at the steady 2.2 percent design rate, giving $478,921.97 in 2030, $595,351.87 in 2040, and $740,086.84 in 2050. Every value in this record is a model value made for the design, and every display of the series must keep the design-study label.

The Problem

What would a median new house cost in 2050 if it rose at a steady 2.2 percent a year from 2024, and how does that compare with the rate new houses actually rose at over the last sixty-one years?

The Worked Solution

The design takes the real Census median new-home price of $420,300 in 2024 and grows it forward at the steady 2.2 percent design rate, giving $478,921.97 in 2030, $595,351.87 in 2040, and $740,086.84 in 2050. The design shows the real house history beside the model, from $18,000 in 1963 to $420,300 in 2024, and notes that the real rate over that span was about 5.3 percent a year, much faster than the model rate. The series is 2024: $420,300 per house; 2030: $478,922 per house; 2040: $595,352 per house; 2050: $740,087 per house. The design projects prices forward from its 2024 anchor at a steady 2.2% nominal rise per year, a Signature design assumption, not a forecast. This is a design-study record: every value here is a model value made for the design, not a real historical price. The demo recomputes the checkable part of this solution from the key figures {"points": 4, "earliest_year": 2024, "latest_year": 2050, "per_house_first": 420300.0, "per_house_last": 740086.84, "per_house_multiple": 1.76, "per_house_cagr": 0.022}.

The Answer

The model house cost $420,300 in 2024 and $740,086.84 in 2050, a nominal multiple of about 1.76 over twenty-six years, which is a compound annual growth rate of about 2.2 percent, by construction. In 2024 dollars the 2050 model house costs about $411,641, so the model says the house stays almost exactly as dear in real terms, because the model rate and the model deflator are nearly the same by design. The series is 2024: $420,300 per house; 2030: $478,922 per house; 2040: $595,352 per house; 2050: $740,087 per house. From 2024 to 2050 the per house price rose from $420,300 to $740,087, a multiple of 1.76 and a compound annual growth rate of 2.2% per year.

When It Applies

When designing a price tool, a classroom exhibit, or a then-versus-now display that needs a clean, labeled model series to work from.

Limits

As a design-study, this record proves nothing about real future prices. The steady 2.2% model rise is a smooth design assumption; real prices jump, stall, and fall, and no model series here should be quoted as a forecast. As a design-study, this record proves nothing about real future prices. The steady 2.2 percent model rise is a smooth design assumption; real house prices jump, stall, and fall, and no model series here should be quoted as a forecast. Houses are not loaves of bread: a new house in 2050 will be bigger, better equipped, and built under different rules than a new house in 2024, so the model price compares different things.

Math Proof

The same arithmetic as the public records, applied to the design's model series: the nominal multiple is M = P_last / P_first; the compound annual growth rate over N years is CAGR = M^(1/N) - 1; a model price restated in 2024 dollars is P_2024 = P_year x 313.7 / D_year, where D_year is the Signature model deflator for that year (2024: 313.7; 2030: 381.0; 2040: 464.0; 2050: 564.0) or the real CPI-U for a historical anchor year. From 2024 to 2050 the per house price rose from $420,300 to $740,087, a multiple of 1.76 and a compound annual growth rate of 2.2% per year. The demo recomputes all three from the key figures and prints them twice with SHA-256 digests. Formal statement: P_2024 = P_year * 313.7 / D_year; CAGR = (P_last / P_first)^(1/N) - 1. P_year is the model price in the stated year and unit; D_year is the Signature model deflator for that year, or the real CPI-U annual average for a historical anchor year; 313.7 is the CPI-U annual average for 2024; N is the number of years between the first and last model price in the same unit.

Science Proof

The design uses the CPI-U as its reference frame: the real CPI-U annual averages for historical anchor years, and the Signature model deflator for projected years. The deflator is a design assumption, stated in the method, not a measurement and not a forecast. The design uses the CPI-U as its reference frame: the real CPI-U annual averages for historical anchor years, and the Signature model deflator for projected years. The deflator is a design assumption, stated in the method, not a measurement and not a forecast. The demo recomputes the nominal multiple, the compound annual growth rate, and the 2024-dollar restatement for every model price from the key figures. Working demo program

import hashlib
import math
series = [(2024, 420300.0, 'per house'), (2030, 478921.97, 'per house'), (2040, 595351.87, 'per house'), (2050, 740086.84, 'per house')]
cpi = {1930: 16.7, 1940: 14.0, 1950: 24.1, 1960: 29.6, 1970: 38.8, 1980: 82.4, 1990: 130.7, 2000: 172.2, 2010: 218.1, 2020: 258.8, 2024: 313.7, 2030: 381.0, 2040: 464.0, 2050: 564.0}
def compute():
    groups = {}
    for (y, p, u) in series:
        groups.setdefault(u, []).append((y, p))
    out = {}
    for u in sorted(groups):
        pts = groups[u]
        y0, p0 = pts[0]
        y1, p1 = pts[-1]
        key = u.replace(' ', '_').replace('-', '_').replace('/', '_').replace('.', '')
        out[key + '_first'] = p0
        out[key + '_last'] = p1
        out[key + '_multiple'] = round(p1 / p0, 2)
        out[key + '_span_years'] = y1 - y0
        out[key + '_cagr'] = round((p1 / p0) ** (1.0 / (y1 - y0)) - 1.0, 4) if y1 > y0 else 0.0
    conv = {}
    for (y, p, u) in series:
        if y in cpi:
            conv[y] = round(p * 313.7 / cpi[y], 2)
    out['in_2024_dollars'] = conv
    out['earliest_year'] = series[0][0]
    out['latest_year'] = series[-1][0]
    out['points'] = len(series)
    return out
outputs = compute()
result = "|".join(f"{k}={v}" for k, v in sorted(outputs.items()))
h1 = hashlib.sha256(result.encode()).hexdigest()
outputs2 = compute()
result2 = "|".join(f"{k}={v}" for k, v in sorted(outputs2.items()))
h2 = hashlib.sha256(result2.encode()).hexdigest()
for k, v in sorted(outputs.items()):
    print(f"{k}: {v}")
print(f"Result: {result}")
print(f"Run 1 SHA-256: {h1}")
print(f"Run 2 SHA-256: {h2}")
print(f"Deterministic: {'MATCH' if h1 == h2 else 'MISMATCH'}")

Full working program

import hashlib
class SignatureHouse2050Program:
    """Working program for this record; run() recomputes the demo result."""
    def run(self):
        import hashlib
        import math
        series = [(2024, 420300.0, 'per house'), (2030, 478921.97, 'per house'), (2040, 595351.87, 'per house'), (2050, 740086.84, 'per house')]
        cpi = {1930: 16.7, 1940: 14.0, 1950: 24.1, 1960: 29.6, 1970: 38.8, 1980: 82.4, 1990: 130.7, 2000: 172.2, 2010: 218.1, 2020: 258.8, 2024: 313.7, 2030: 381.0, 2040: 464.0, 2050: 564.0}
        def compute():
            groups = {}
            for (y, p, u) in series:
                groups.setdefault(u, []).append((y, p))
            out = {}
            for u in sorted(groups):
                pts = groups[u]
                y0, p0 = pts[0]
                y1, p1 = pts[-1]
                key = u.replace(' ', '_').replace('-', '_').replace('/', '_').replace('.', '')
                out[key + '_first'] = p0
                out[key + '_last'] = p1
                out[key + '_multiple'] = round(p1 / p0, 2)
                out[key + '_span_years'] = y1 - y0
                out[key + '_cagr'] = round((p1 / p0) ** (1.0 / (y1 - y0)) - 1.0, 4) if y1 > y0 else 0.0
            conv = {}
            for (y, p, u) in series:
                if y in cpi:
                    conv[y] = round(p * 313.7 / cpi[y], 2)
            out['in_2024_dollars'] = conv
            out['earliest_year'] = series[0][0]
            out['latest_year'] = series[-1][0]
            out['points'] = len(series)
            return out
        outputs = compute()
        result = "|".join(f"{k}={v}" for k, v in sorted(outputs.items()))
        h1 = hashlib.sha256(result.encode()).hexdigest()
        outputs2 = compute()
        result2 = "|".join(f"{k}={v}" for k, v in sorted(outputs2.items()))
        h2 = hashlib.sha256(result2.encode()).hexdigest()
        for k, v in sorted(outputs.items()):
            print(f"{k}: {v}")
        print(f"Result: {result}")
        print(f"Run 1 SHA-256: {h1}")
        print(f"Run 2 SHA-256: {h2}")
        print(f"Deterministic: {'MATCH' if h1 == h2 else 'MISMATCH'}")
        return {"deterministic": h1 == h2, "result": result}
if __name__ == "__main__":
    print(SignatureHouse2050Program().run())

Signature model series, design-study values; historical anchors from the cited public background sources.

Verification path

Execute demo.code with python3 and compare stdout to expected_output byte for byte; then run the program class the same way. Re-check the cited sources: each URL must return HTTP 200, and each price figure must match the year and source named in the record.

Formal equation

P_2024 = P_year * 313.7 / D_year; CAGR = (P_last / P_first)^(1/N) - 1

Formal equation, in plain words

P_year is the model price in the stated year and unit; D_year is the Signature model deflator for that year, or the real CPI-U annual average for a historical anchor year; 313.7 is the CPI-U annual average for 2024; N is the number of years between the first and last model price in the same unit.

Formal status

stated, not machine-checked

Scientific equation

Signature model deflator, design-study values (2024: 313.7; 2030: 381.0; 2040: 464.0; 2050: 564.0)

Proof sketch

The demo recomputes the nominal multiple, the compound annual growth rate, and the 2024-dollar restatement for every model price from the key figures. The cited background sources confirm the real historical anchors the design builds on; the model values themselves are checked only for internal consistency.

Proof status

The arithmetic is machine-checked by the demo; the scientific claim is stated, not machine-checked.

Results reported

The model car cost $33,332 in 2024 and $58,692.78 in 2050, a nominal multiple of about 1.76 over twenty-six years, which is a compound annual growth rate of about 2.2 percent, by construction. In 2024 dollars the 2050 model car costs about $32,645, so the model says the car stays almost exactly as dear in real terms, because the model rate and the model deflator are nearly the same by design. The series is 2024: $33,332 per car; 2030: $37,981 per car; 2040: $47,215 per car; 2050: $58,693 per car. From 2024 to 2050 the per car price rose from $33,332 to $58,693, a multiple of 1.76 and a compound annual growth rate of 2.2% per year.

Skeptical analysis

As a design-study, this record proves nothing about real future prices. The steady 2.2% model rise is a smooth design assumption; real prices jump, stall, and fall, and no model series here should be quoted as a forecast. As a design-study, this record proves nothing about real future prices. The steady 2.2 percent model rise is a smooth design assumption; real car prices jump, stall, and fall, and no model series here should be quoted as a forecast. Cars are not loaves of bread: a new car in 2050 will carry technology that does not exist yet, and the BEA car-only series must not be mixed with the Kelley Blue Book average transaction price, which includes trucks and SUVs.

Specification

  • dimensions: model price in US dollars per per car; model years 2024-2050; Signature model deflator points
  • care: Never present a model value as a real historical price or a forecast; keep the design-study label on every display of the series.

Boundless scales

  • A1: One 1 Signature car 2050 cost design in the design: what the model charges and why.
  • A2: The whole model series: the design across its decades.
  • A3: The design against the whole economy: model prices restated in 2024 dollars.

Key figures

  • points: 4
  • earliest_year: 2024
  • latest_year: 2050
  • per_car_first: 33332.0
  • per_car_last: 58692.78
  • per_car_multiple: 1.76
  • per_car_cagr: 0.022

Working demo program

import hashlib
import math
series = [(2024, 33332.0, 'per car'), (2030, 37981.03, 'per car'), (2040, 47214.53, 'per car'), (2050, 58692.78, 'per car')]
cpi = {1930: 16.7, 1940: 14.0, 1950: 24.1, 1960: 29.6, 1970: 38.8, 1980: 82.4, 1990: 130.7, 2000: 172.2, 2010: 218.1, 2020: 258.8, 2024: 313.7, 2030: 381.0, 2040: 464.0, 2050: 564.0}
def compute():
    groups = {}
    for (y, p, u) in series:
        groups.setdefault(u, []).append((y, p))
    out = {}
    for u in sorted(groups):
        pts = groups[u]
        y0, p0 = pts[0]
        y1, p1 = pts[-1]
        key = u.replace(' ', '_').replace('-', '_').replace('/', '_').replace('.', '')
        out[key + '_first'] = p0
        out[key + '_last'] = p1
        out[key + '_multiple'] = round(p1 / p0, 2)
        out[key + '_span_years'] = y1 - y0
        out[key + '_cagr'] = round((p1 / p0) ** (1.0 / (y1 - y0)) - 1.0, 4) if y1 > y0 else 0.0
    conv = {}
    for (y, p, u) in series:
        if y in cpi:
            conv[y] = round(p * 313.7 / cpi[y], 2)
    out['in_2024_dollars'] = conv
    out['earliest_year'] = series[0][0]
    out['latest_year'] = series[-1][0]
    out['points'] = len(series)
    return out
outputs = compute()
result = "|".join(f"{k}={v}" for k, v in sorted(outputs.items()))
h1 = hashlib.sha256(result.encode()).hexdigest()
outputs2 = compute()
result2 = "|".join(f"{k}={v}" for k, v in sorted(outputs2.items()))
h2 = hashlib.sha256(result2.encode()).hexdigest()
for k, v in sorted(outputs.items()):
    print(f"{k}: {v}")
print(f"Result: {result}")
print(f"Run 1 SHA-256: {h1}")
print(f"Run 2 SHA-256: {h2}")
print(f"Deterministic: {'MATCH' if h1 == h2 else 'MISMATCH'}")

Full working program

import hashlib
class SignatureCar2050Program:
    """Working program for this record; run() recomputes the demo result."""
    def run(self):
        import hashlib
        import math
        series = [(2024, 33332.0, 'per car'), (2030, 37981.03, 'per car'), (2040, 47214.53, 'per car'), (2050, 58692.78, 'per car')]
        cpi = {1930: 16.7, 1940: 14.0, 1950: 24.1, 1960: 29.6, 1970: 38.8, 1980: 82.4, 1990: 130.7, 2000: 172.2, 2010: 218.1, 2020: 258.8, 2024: 313.7, 2030: 381.0, 2040: 464.0, 2050: 564.0}
        def compute():
            groups = {}
            for (y, p, u) in series:
                groups.setdefault(u, []).append((y, p))
            out = {}
            for u in sorted(groups):
                pts = groups[u]
                y0, p0 = pts[0]
                y1, p1 = pts[-1]
                key = u.replace(' ', '_').replace('-', '_').replace('/', '_').replace('.', '')
                out[key + '_first'] = p0
                out[key + '_last'] = p1
                out[key + '_multiple'] = round(p1 / p0, 2)
                out[key + '_span_years'] = y1 - y0
                out[key + '_cagr'] = round((p1 / p0) ** (1.0 / (y1 - y0)) - 1.0, 4) if y1 > y0 else 0.0
            conv = {}
            for (y, p, u) in series:
                if y in cpi:
                    conv[y] = round(p * 313.7 / cpi[y], 2)
            out['in_2024_dollars'] = conv
            out['earliest_year'] = series[0][0]
            out['latest_year'] = series[-1][0]
            out['points'] = len(series)
            return out
        outputs = compute()
        result = "|".join(f"{k}={v}" for k, v in sorted(outputs.items()))
        h1 = hashlib.sha256(result.encode()).hexdigest()
        outputs2 = compute()
        result2 = "|".join(f"{k}={v}" for k, v in sorted(outputs2.items()))
        h2 = hashlib.sha256(result2.encode()).hexdigest()
        for k, v in sorted(outputs.items()):
            print(f"{k}: {v}")
        print(f"Result: {result}")
        print(f"Run 1 SHA-256: {h1}")
        print(f"Run 2 SHA-256: {h2}")
        print(f"Deterministic: {'MATCH' if h1 == h2 else 'MISMATCH'}")
        return {"deterministic": h1 == h2, "result": result}
if __name__ == "__main__":
    print(SignatureCar2050Program().run())
